on behalf of the pse group. mr president, commissioner, the revision of the basic guidelines on state aid to public service broadcasting, which was initiated by the french presidency, is a very timely debate both within many member states and for the position of the media at european and international level. at the same time, however, the revision of the telecommunications package, which essentially restructures the way and criteria for allocating spectrum at european level, shows that the rules governing the financing of audiovisual providers are now of fundamental importance for the future regulation or deregulation of services related to the information society, innovation, as well as public and economic interest services. this is the political question we are essentially called upon to solve. it is clear that the discussion on state funding is in principle focused on the so-called public service broadcasters, as defined by each member state, given the substantially smaller resources at their disposal, but above all the important public interest role assigned to them. the important role of broadcasting organisations is mentioned in the unesco convention, the amsterdam protocol and the 2001 commission communication. the commission has already set up a platform where the role of the public media is clearly defined. but the state aid debate has been opened. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i would like to mention a few points: first, the question of the definition of the public service remit should not be confused with the question of the financial mechanism chosen for the provision of those services. while public television may engage in commercial activities, such as selling advertising time, in order to achieve certain revenues, this has been condemned by some governments, such as the french government, and is reversed under national law. on the other hand, commercial broadcasters subject to public service obligations also play an important role. the coexistence of private and public service providers is a key component of the european market. i would like to close with one more thing: the commission's proposed ex ante state aid examination procedures should be of concern not because subsidiarity is postponed or overlapped, but because they must be examined as to whether they are compatible with the state aid conditions. finally, it should be noted that the issue of national aid to radio stations and the media should be examined in the context of international relations, because european organizations have to compete with international giants and strict legislation will damage the european achievement and the community acquis.
mr president, commissioner, as the undersigned colleagues in the oral question have pointed out, and as distinguished speakers have said in detail at the public hearing in brussels last week, we must all see public television as a means of providing information to the public which will, to a large extent, meet its operational obligations. the public debate, the independent supervisory authority and the diversity of programmes, in accordance with the history and culture of each member state, need to be protected in the light of the development of new technologies and the number of organisations involved on a daily basis. in the context of healthy competition, the commission needs to identify new ways of protecting the public interest and public television. in particular, the public service mission of broadcasters must be clearly defined; to improve the assessment of state aid; the commission's prior assessment of the impact of the directive on the free movement of goods and services should not be subject to unnecessary administrative and financial burdens on member states and public service broadcasters. more effective control mechanisms must also be established in the new media environment. moreover, since public broadcasting plays a decisive role in cultural and linguistic diversity, all these media, but also public organisations and television, must provide quality programmes and, at the same time, in competition with other relevant operators, must be able to respond positively to the challenges of the times by broadcasting events of global interest, such as the olympic games, the world cup, etc., which today, unfortunately, tends to be overturned by the fact that public television, as private operators have more money, cannot cope with the costs. the citizens will therefore not be able to watch these events along with the rest of the programmes. in conclusion, i would also like to point out that the current market offers and the interests of the commercial media in determining the scope of competence and activity of public service broadcasters are not, in the same way, related to the general interest, with a few natural exceptions. the commission should take this seriously.
member of the commission. − mr president, the commission welcomes the report prepared by ms mcguinness and the intense debate which has taken place in the various ep committees on the elements surrounding this very topical subject, which range from trade to biofuels, price monitoring, investment policy, the financial crisis, climate change and water use in agriculture. the commission shares the broad analysis on the causes of the food crisis which hit many developing countries in the first part of 2008. the commission will continue to analyse the link between food prices and energy prices. the causality issue is a highly complex one as it involves the interplay of a number of demand and supply factors. energy prices constitute only one of these factors but one which has both a direct and indirect impact. the topic of biofuels was discussed in detail during the various ep sessions. there is a clear difference between eu and us policy on the extent to which cereal production is being diverted to biofuels. eu biofuels policy does not reduce the availability of food, as the quantities of feedstocks used are very small on a global scale. even if the eu moves closer to its 10% target, the impact on food prices will be limited, in particular for two reasons: first, more and more biofuels will come from non-food feedstocks or will be made from residues or waste. second, the efficiency of techniques for producing biofuels will further improve, and average yields can also be expected to continue to increase. a sustainable eu biofuels policy is overall a pro-poor policy. it will provide additional opportunities for the two thirds of the world’s poor who live in rural areas and thus depend on a thriving agricultural sector. however, not all groups will benefit to the same extent. the commission is committed to closely monitoring the effects on food security and on food prices. the eu has already taken steps in order to address global food security by adapting the cap to the changed market and global situation. the health check which was recently agreed will modernise, simplify and streamline the cap and remove restrictions on farmers, thus helping them to respond better to signals from the market and to face new challenges. the agreement on the health check abolishes arable set-aside, increases milk quotas gradually leading up to their abolition in 2015, and converts market intervention into a genuine safety net. new challenges, such as climate change, water management, renewable energy and biodiversity, which will have an impact on developing countries, have also been addressed in it. the cap will not remain static after 2013, and the preliminary debate has already been engaged since the informal agricultural ministers meeting in annecy in september. the future cap has to be seen in the context of a broader vision in which sustainable development, competitiveness and global food balances will form an integral part. various high-level events have placed global food security high on the international agenda. there is a clear understanding and recognition internationally to place agriculture and rural development higher on the policy agenda at national, regional and possibly at continental level. for example, we have discussed in detail the topic of agriculture and food security during our college-to-college meeting with the african union in october, a debate which we intend to intensify in the course of this year. last but not least, the commission needs to follow up on the g8 leaders’ statement on global food security. in the run-up to the adoption by the council of the food facility on 16 december, the european commission has already had very fruitful discussions with the un high-level task force. the european commission looks forward to the implementation of the comprehensive framework for action. the commission is convinced that the global partnership on agriculture and food security which is gradually taking shape will have a key role in implementing the various recommendations made in this report, including how to support smallholder farmer production and what type of trade policies should be adopted to contribute to food security, especially in the most vulnerable communities. evidently, export restrictions and bans should be avoided and the way forward is more rather than less trade liberalisation. enhanced trade flows are part of the solution towards food security. the commission hopes that the bold step taken by the european institutions to mobilise the one billion euros which will complement other financial instruments which have been earmarked to respond to the food crisis – of both a short- and medium- to long-term nature – is followed up by other donors. the madrid conference on food security for all on 26 and 27 january is certainly a key event to move the debate to another level, one that seeks to identify the key priorities to address world food hunger. the european commission will continue to play a proactive role in contributing to global food security and the mcguinness report has certainly been a good analysis to look at some possible avenues which could be considered by the ec and the broader international community.
in writing. the international and regional agreements have so far proved insufficient to achieve market and trade supply harmonization, transparency and price stability for agricultural products. the regulation of agricultural markets should be based on a long-term strategy of effective measures and on the organisation and information of producers on the situation and prospects of the markets. the basic principle of such a policy is to establish an income safety net against risks and crises arising either from adverse natural phenomena or from market distortions and unusually long and extensive price falls. integrated and effective policies are needed, such as: - european and international production and market monitoring systems for early warning and determination of production trends; - a global inventory of food and food stocks; - a european market monitoring system for recording changes in prices of agricultural products and input costs, which can be combined with an international equivalent within the fao. it is also desirable that the future doha round agreement should include emergency situations in which food aid is allowed because the provisions so far are not binding.
in writing. the ec's solemn meeting on the 10th anniversary of the un in a period of deep capitalist crisis is followed by repeated ec resolutions and reports on the same subject and is another attempt to gild the pill of the anti-people, anti-worker policy of the eu, which, after the upheavals in the socialist countries, proceeded to the maastricht treaty and the full-scale attack on workers' rights and freedoms. the un, the creation of the ekt and the introduction of the euro are necessary links in the chain of capitalist restructuring that capital is promoting in order to defend itself against workers' demands, to ensure its profitability through the intensification of the exploitation of the working class, the popular strata. the arguments for price stability and reducing inflation, protecting economies from risks and crises have proved futile. the un protects the profits of monopolies, facilitates privatizations - mergers. in this decade, workers have seen their incomes shrink, their employment relationships deteriorate, their insurance rights evaporate, health and education services deteriorate, they are commodified and privatized. the workers reject the euro-road and its supporters, the lisbon strategy and the lisbon treaty and fight against the un and the eu itself for popular power and economy.
in writing. the world food problem, instead of shrinking, is growing and affects all layers of the population not only in the least developed countries but also in the most developed. this is mainly because the main criterion for the production of agri-food products is profit and not the world's food needs. the inclusion of food in international stock exchanges has resulted in skyrocketing prices and, accordingly, profits for multinational food companies, a deliberate reduction in agricultural production and global food stocks, and an increase in the number of undernourished people. in order to deal with this unacceptable situation, which condemns 1 billion people to malnutrition and starvation, the report confines itself to wishes that are cancelled out by its obsession with continuing the same policy: support for the cap, its revisions and 'diagnostic control', the conclusion of the wto negotiations, the decoupling of aid from production, the continuation of the production of biofuels, under the pretext of the environment, using land that could be used for food production. it does not even set out the principles of food sovereignty and security, the right to food self-sufficiency. the report also calls for the eu to take action to combat the spread of the virus, and for the eu to take action to combat the spread of the virus.
in writing. the report on public finances to the un endorses the anti-union decisions of the council and the commission which aim to strengthen the competitiveness of monopoly enterprises in order to safeguard the profits of capital by shifting the burden of the deep capitalist crisis onto the backs of the workers. the anti-people framework that the eu has established, through the stability pact and the lisbon strategy, for the conduct of the budgetary policies of the member states, particularly the un, is being strengthened. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to carcinogens at work. the criticisms that the huge sums allocated to the crisis are not reaching smes and, even more so, workers are false. the outdated and failed models of state intervention that cover up market failures are wishful thinking and an attempt to disorient workers by seeking social consent in a rotten system. the only solution is the workers' struggle for popular power and popular economy to overthrow capitalist barbarity.
mr president, the current dominance of parliament's view on the council's attempt to impose the biometric record of six-year-olds is a victory over the fundamental principle that personal data may only be collected if it is proved necessary, proportionate and of course useful to do so; a principle which i fear has been frequently ignored in recent years by the council and the commission in their legislative initiatives. in the case of children's passports and fingerprints, it's obvious that children need their own passports with their biometric data in an effort to prevent kidnappings, child pornography, trafficking of children. but at the same time it is obviously illegal to collect this data if it is not necessary. and as far as fingerprints go, we have studies that show that for 6-year-olds, they're not even useful. their fingerprints change so quickly that passports and identification through them become useless. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. it requires the commission to undertake a serious study to see when children can be protected from their fingerprints and it allows fingerprints to be taken only at ages when we know for sure that this is the case. finally, in any case, as regards biometric data in passports, we say 'yes' to identifying the holder; 'yes' for its identification; the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
mr president, i too would like to congratulate the author of the report, mr lambsdorff, but also the shadow rapporteurs on the great work they have done. the main characteristics of the european market are the fragmentation at national level. the export of products related to this defence and security sector is subject to national authorisation systems which differ in terms of procedure, scope and deadlines. this new legislation promotes transparency and lays the foundations for the creation of a single open european defence equipment market, which is also a key factor in strengthening european security and defence policy. it is also important that there be substantive provisions on security of supply and information. the use of article 296 of the ec treaty is also clarified, but is now limited to actual derogations as provided for in the treaty and as requested by the european court of justice. in conclusion, i would like to stress the importance of the role of small and mediumsized enterprises, which in some member states are a sector employing thousands of people, being strengthened through flexible regulations.

the commission's vice-president - madam president, i am answering to mr aylward. the precondition for an effective fight against terrorism and other forms of serious crime is to ensure that the right people have access to the right information at the right time, which is a huge challenge at the level of the european union. we have tried to promote and facilitate the effective exchange of information between police services in the member states. the framework decision of 18 december 2006, known as the swedish decision, which all member states should have implemented by mid-december 2008, i.e. very recently, creates a common legal framework for the rapid exchange of information and intelligence between member states' law enforcement authorities. this framework decision requires that the police services of the member state receiving a request process that request using criteria which are in line with those applied to national requests. that's the first thing. the second is the council decision of 23 june 2008, known as the prüm decision. this decision provides for a detailed mechanism for the exchange of specific categories of data: fingerprints, dna profiles, vehicle registration information, all in order to be able to carry out criminal investigations. under the terms of this prüm council decision, member states give each other limited access to their dna and fingerprint databases in order to be able to check whether any matches exist. this is very interesting because this council decision allows for an extremely efficient exchange of dna and fingerprints. europol, for its part, has a crucial role to play. europol's information system, the etias, allows member states to determine whether law enforcement authorities in other member states have the information they need at operational level. of course, for europol to be able to play this role, the member states must supply this europol information system in a satisfactory way. now we will obviously have to make new arrangements for this exchange of information in the next five-year programme which will succeed the hague programme. this programme will have to ensure a coordinated, coherent approach to information exchange. we must include in this five-year programme a european union strategy on information management. but of course, information sharing also means protecting personal data. the strategy should allow for a comprehensive approach to information exchange that meets the needs of police services and is based on the interoperability of information technology systems. that is the substance of my answer to mr aylward.
the commission's vice-president - the seizure of goods resulting from illicit trafficking is currently being studied. judge jean-louis bruguière was in charge of monitoring the swift programme, the programme to track down terrorist financing. we'll have his conclusions soon. the european union has benefited from information generated by the terrorist financing tracking programme and mr justice bruguière had to verify that all this was done in compliance with data protection. i can say that this is likely to facilitate the confiscation of the assets of a number of people who have made illicit profits. on the exchange of information, i told you that the europol information system could be much more efficient if the member states provided information in a more reliable and transparent way. we'll work on it. it is one of my main concerns to establish this trust between the various intelligence authorities in the member states so that we can move towards much more efficient exchanges. i have not heard of any exception clause in this area. i think i pretty much answered the questions. threats to nuclear power stations: we now also have a text on critical infrastructure, of which nuclear power stations are obviously one, and on which the commission has proposed a text which allows for better monitoring of critical infrastructure.
the commission's vice-president - i answer to mr. france. the eu's 2005 global approach to migration aimed to provide a more appropriate response to the challenges posed by migration to the eu as a whole. this comprehensive approach is based on enhancing dialogue and cooperation with third countries in all dimensions of migration, with a view to establishing a partnership for better migration management. in order to give concrete substance to this comprehensive approach to migration, the european commission supports cooperation initiatives with third countries in the fields of migration and asylum. for example, the aeneas programme, which funded more than 100 projects in the period 2004-2006, or its successor, migration and asylum, which had a budget of €205 million for the period 2007-2010. many of the initiatives selected in an annual call for proposals are proposed and implemented by member states in collaboration with third countries. for example, on the basis of the aeneas programme, the commission is financing a spanish-moroccan project to manage seasonal immigration between the provinces of ben slimane in morocco and huelva in spain. the programme also supports cooperation between spain and colombia to develop circular migration. we also financed the temporary return to cape verde of highly qualified cape verdean nationals present in portugal to carry out information and training activities among potential candidates for emigration. in addition to these interventions, geographical financial instruments, the european development fund, the european neighbourhood and partnership instruments, also contribute to this comprehensive approach to migration. for example, the european commission recently supported the establishment of the migration information and management centre in mali, a project in which some eu member states are closely involved. the commission also proposed new tools to promote partnership with third countries and to develop greater synergies between community and member state action. we now have mobility partnerships, which are a new tool that the european union is putting in place, for now on a pilot basis. these mobility partnerships allow the development of dialogue and cooperation between the union and third countries in the field of legal migration, development, prevention and reduction of illegal immigration. we have signed the first partnerships between the european union and cape verde and between the european union and the republic of moldova. in these partnerships we have made concrete offers of cooperation. for example, in the framework of the partnership with cape verde, portugal has proposed to sign a new protocol extending the scope of an existing protocol on the temporary migration of cape verdean workers for the purpose of providing work in portugal. other tools are migration reports, which are analyses of the migration situation in a given country, cooperation platforms to bring together representatives of the country and the main donors involved in migration in the third country concerned. a cooperation platform has been set up in ethiopia at the initiative of the united kingdom, and another is being considered in south africa. finally, the integration fund and the return fund can of course help the member states to put in place pre-departure measures in third countries which will facilitate the professional, civic and cultural integration of the candidate for emigration in the countries of destination, or, conversely, short-term support measures for returnees. i have given you a number of examples, but above all i would like to say that i am absolutely convinced that europe needs to manage migration flows by means of a comprehensive approach that links migration and development and that really makes it possible to manage migration in a concerted way. i believe that this is what we must strive for and that this will make europe's management of migration an example for the world.
the commission's vice-president - first of all, i would like to reply to mr france that i will of course now use all my strength to develop the overall approach through mobility partnerships between the whole union and third countries. you are right, there are some member states which have succeeded in these bilateral agreements, but this must also be the case for the whole of europe, and you were right to stress the risks of illegal immigration in the absence of a good organisation of legal migration. secondly, the case you are referring to, mr president, is a case which shows that the return policy must be conducted with discernment. it is not a question of sending people back to third countries where their lives and lives will be threatened. so, then, discernment is needed. the return directive, i will not go into it, has been controversial, but it does allow, if it is transposed correctly in the member states, to monitor a little bit how these return policies are carried out. i intend to do so with care and with care.
member of the commission. − firstly i should clarify that the commission does not pursue as a general objective the opening of closed professions. however, in the context of its internal market policy, it considers that the legitimate need for member states to regulate certain activities has to be reconciled with the need to ensure the free movement of professionals throughout europe. it goes without saying that better quality and wider choice in professional services must have a positive effect on the entire eu economy. to this end, the services directive obliges member states to screen their national legislation for requirements applied to certain professions, such as quantitative and territorial restrictions, in order to evaluate their appropriateness in the light of conditions established in the case-law of the european court of justice. by 28 december 2009 member states will have to present to the commission their report on the results of this screening and evaluation exercise. this will give them an opportunity to identify the possible negative repercussions of opening up certain closed professions and justify any restrictions. on the basis of these national reports, the commission and all member states will undertake the process of mutual evaluation and will examine and discuss changes introduced in member states’ legislation as well as any requirements that would have been maintained. on 28 december 2010 the commission will present a summary report to the european parliament and the council on the results of this mutual evaluation process. obviously, requirements to think about specifically regulating the professions will be discussed in this context. in addition, again with the aim of ensuring the functioning of the internal market, infringement proceedings have been opened whenever national rules are found to impose discriminatory or disproportionate restrictions in the area of regulated professions as regards, for instance, access to ownership, incompatibilities or compulsory tariffs. finally, the commission’s work in the field of competition also seeks to stimulate appropriate patterns of review of the existing professional rules within each member state, as provided for in the two commission reports on competition in professional services. national competition authorities are invited to check, in the light of competition rules, whether a regulation has clearly defined both a public interest objective and the least restrictive means of achieving that objective.
the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one. on the other hand, there are certain professions, which i call 'closed', for which money has been paid. i am referring to taxi licences, which are subject to a fee in many member states of europe, and to the pharmacy profession, which is practised by means of demographic and defectionist measures. i would like to hear the commissioner's thoughts on this.
member of the commission. − there are two separate issues being spoken about here by the various members who have addressed this subject. one is the scope of the services directive; the other is the regulation of professional qualifications. the services directive has in its scope all of the services in the european union with certain limited exceptions. the question about pharmacists was raised by mr angelakas. he may be aware that we do have some infringement proceedings against some member states in this particular area. on the question of mutual evaluation which is required under the services directive, each member state has to go through all its rules and regulations before 28 december this year and then we will have a mutual evaluation to see what is proportionate and what is not. the question that mrs doyle raises is that of gambling infringements. she will be aware that we have, in one form or another, some actions pertaining to at least 15 member states at this particular time. there has been court jurisprudence on this particular matter. gambling is a service that comes under the scope of the services directive like everything else, and these matters have to be addressed. there are various stages of proceedings against member states in this area. we are also inundated with lots of other possible infringements in this area.
member of the commission. − the commission has closely followed the activity of the credit rating agencies (cras) over the past few years. following the european parliament’s resolution on credit rating agencies in february 2004, the commission considered very carefully what legislative measures would be necessary to regulate the activities of credit rating agencies. in line with the advice received from the committee of european securities regulators (cesr) in march 2005, the commission adopted in 2006 a communication on credit rating agencies. in this communication the commission concluded that various financial services directives, combined with self-regulation by the credit rating agencies, on the basis of the iosco code of conduct, the methods for cras could provide a satisfactory answer to the major issues of concern in relation to credit rating agencies. it is stated that this approach would require continuous monitoring of developments by the commission. in addition, the commission asked cesr to monitor compliance with the iosco code and to report back on an annual basis. at the same time, the commission indicated in the communication that it might consider putting forward legislative action if it became clear that compliance with eu rules or iosco’s code was unsatisfactory or if new circumstances were to arise, including serious problems of market failure or significant changes in the way credit rating agencies are regulated in other parts of the world. the financial crisis shed a new light on cras. since august 2007 the financial markets worldwide suffered from a major confidence crisis. this financial crisis is a complex phenomenon involving multiple actors. the credit rating agencies are close to the origin of the problems that have arisen with subprime markets. the crisis highlighted poor performance by credit rating agencies. an explanation could be found in the unsatisfactory way the agencies managed their conflicts of interest, the lack of quality of the methodologies they issue, insufficient transparency around their activities or the inappropriate internal governance in the agencies. the subprime crisis demonstrated that the framework for the operation of credit rating agencies needs to be significantly reinforced. that is why in june 2008 i announced that the commission would take regulatory action in this area and on 12 november 2008 the college adopted the proposal covering regulatory activity of credit rating agencies with the agreement of this parliament and of the council.

the president-in-office of the council. - thank you for your speech, i would like to start my final speech by thanking you for all the opinions, it was a very friendly reception, i didn't expect it. i would also like to say that our presidency and myself as president of the european council will be in close contact and cooperation with the european commission throughout the six months. the first 14 days have shown that a very permanent, daily and really very diligent contact not only with josé manuel barroso but with the whole commission has ensured a common approach to the problems that we faced at the beginning of the year, and i am here demonstrating not only cooperation with the european parliament but, above all, communication in solving those operational problems with the european commission, and i wanted to thank president barroso. i swore before i came here that i wouldn't joke and one joke i made that would have made us all laugh, you didn't understand, but that's okay, i'll try it again and again and maybe eventually the german translation will be right and maybe we'll understand each other. the lisbon treaty cannot be a mantra. the lisbon treaty must be a means, not an end. it is a means of improving the functioning of the european union. it cannot be enforced in any way. each country has its own tools and rules of the game to achieve ratification. the fact that i signed the contract does not mean that i will influence the decisions of both chambers of the czech parliament, which are autonomous, free and decide for themselves. nor do we intend to influence the decision of the irish people in advance. it is not possible to enforce the treaty in any way, although i think that it is necessary at the moment and will facilitate the functioning of the european union. that's the end of it, and i'm not going to comment further on that because he's made his personal position on it. i liked to quote mr kirkhope, and i will use another churchill quote to illustrate my view of the present: 'there can be no crisis tomorrow, my diary is already full.' i think what we've been doing these first few weeks is showing that we've got our priorities right and that we've been prepared for this. we started to deal with the gas issue in mid-december during the french presidency. i apologize to all members for not answering their specific questions, i will try to generalize the discussion a bit and try to show a rather general attitude to tackling those specific problems. the question of human trafficking, drugs, children, trafficking in children, etc.: just as there are discussions in this parliament on the level of freedom and security, so too there are discussions in our parliament, i.e. the directive or the whole legislative plan within the framework of the council decision to combat human trafficking, we want to accelerate. i'm just saying that we're ready to do that and we want to address the individual elements of those problems. the big debate about nabucco: but let's say, in all seriousness, that nabucco will be an alternative to other transit routes only if it is also an alternative to resources. and this discussion about ukraine - and my czech colleague josef zieleniec has made it very clear - the problem is political and geopolitical, and if we don't give, i would say, ukraine a chance to fix its internal problems, if we don't actually stop the behavior of individual players, whether individuals or companies in the gas market, then we can get ukraine in its pro-european direction, which is of course a geopolitical problem, and we can only speculate about what the goals of this whole crisis are, whether it's just a short-term price increase or it's a much greater pressure to build nord stream, an alternative northern route or it's really preventing the european direction of ukraine, but anyway, these problems are interrelated and it's not just short-term and it's not just energy. if i were to respond to the recommendation of whether europe should be more liberal or more socialist, i would try one more joke and say that i propose a compromise that it should be liberal conservative, but that's the last joke. the czech republic's path towards adopting the euro: i announced on 1 january that the czech republic would announce on 11 november a possible date for accession. my government is the first government to meet the maastricht criteria. i think that this is not a race, i congratulate my slovak colleagues and we will also wait to see how and what effect the financial crisis will have on the implementation of the rules of the stability and growth pact and on the implementation of virtually all the rules that govern the euro area. i am afraid, and this is also true for the solution to the financial crisis, that diluting the european union's own rules is destructive, and therefore when we talk about europe of rules, we will of course insist on compliance with the directives on public support, on competition, and this will be one of the criteria for assessing all proposals in the context of the solution to the financial crisis. rules are good times and bad times, and that goes for everyone. so this equality has to be absolute. it is a commentary on a europe without barriers: yes, it is intended in at least three senses. in the economic sense, it is the removal of the barriers of the single internal market, in the mental, psychological sense, the removal of the barriers in the minds of individual europeans, which of course the newly acceding countries are aiming at, and also the removal of external barriers, the avoidance of protectionism, the genuine liberalisation of world trade as one of the instruments for solving the crisis, the increase in demand and the actual implementation of the conclusions of the g20 in washington. i might want to say one thing that affects me personally and has already been in the media. vaclav havel is in the hospital. he's very ill. he is a man who symbolizes our pre-october, but also our semi-october orientation, and not only of the czech republic, but symbolizes the fall of the iron curtain. he was the first czech to speak in this european parliament, and i would like to wish him a speedy recovery on behalf of us all. a few very specific responses to the member of this house. i was very moved by mr rouček's concern about the czech government, and i could give at least six examples from the last ten to fifteen years when the countries that held the presidency of the european union had a lot of internal problems and the presidency was successful. it was at the time of the entry into force of the maastricht treaty in belgium, when the state structure was changing and yet the presidency was not affected, it was at the time of the french presidency, when the european union was enlarged to include sweden, finland and austria and when there was an internal political crisis, it was at the time of the treaty of amsterdam in 1999 during the german presidency, when gerhard schröder had big problems and lafontaine left this party, it was at the time of the introduction of the euro during the spanish presidency of josé maría aznar, it was at the time of the irish presidency and i could go on. don't worry about the internal problems of czech politics, the czech presidency won't be affected. nuclear forum: the debate on nuclear cannot be such that the dispute that exists here between the greens, liberals, conservatives, etc., should have some forced outcome. the nuclear forum, which we are organising in cooperation with the european commission in bratislava and prague, should set up a new discussion on security, opportunities, risks, necessity and all the things that have become a bit taboo in recent years. unblocking this taboo is actually the goal of this slovak-czech nuclear forum. why prime minister fico is in ukraine and moscow is obvious, the problems of bulgaria and slovakia are fatal, they are countries that are 100% dependent on gas supplies from ukraine. bulgaria can only draw from the reservoir a third of the power it needs, slovakia already has limited production in thousands of enterprises, its thermal power plant in novatsia has burned down, it is in a rather very crisis situation and i am in daily contact with robert fic and i support his mission, although i do not think it will have a result at the moment. we have to be substantially tougher on both players, on ukraine and on russia, on naftogaz and on gazprom. and i believe that the next steps must be much more effective and more firm both on the part of the european commission and on the part of the czech presidency. we may have to look for tools to overcome some technical problems. it is not possible that for a completely secondary reason, namely the use of a technological gas, these deliveries have not been started, i.e. that we will take further steps in this direction. we didn't talk much about the balkans and related issues and a lot of questions were directed in that direction. mr peterle knows very well that in order to unlock the chapters of the croatian accession process, a bilateral solution to the slovenian-croatian conflict must be found. this is not a european dispute, although it is beginning to interfere with accession talks. in this sense, my contacts with both prime ministers, both presidents and i would say personal input into this problem will perhaps help solve the slovenian-croatian border problems. there is much more i would like to say, however i will try not to delay you any longer, i will try to allow your further proceedings. i would like to say, in conclusion, that we greatly appreciate the possibility of belonging to a community of states that was founded on those values and on those foundations which we can take back as our values and as our objectives after november 1989. we also appreciate the opportunity to preside over this entire community. it's something so unique that my generation, who was 33 in 1989, never expected to live to see it. we also value the ability to solve a lot of problems, and what we value most of all is the freedom of internal discussion. the freedom that allows us to articulate those problems in the spectrum of those opinions that are about to be solved. i can clearly state that tomáš garrigue masaryk said that "democracy is a discussion" and i want to follow in the footsteps of this first czechoslovak president and i am completely open to this discussion. thank you for your attention today, and i look forward to the next one.
president of the commission. − madam president, this has been a very interesting discussion, and i am encouraged by the common understanding of the challenges and generally speaking by the support given to the czech presidency. once again i reiterate my wish to work loyally, constructively and in the best spirit, with our czech friends. their success is also europe’s success. as some of you have underlined, this is the last presidency before the european parliament elections. i think it is very important that during these months we all show how relevant all our institutions are to the wellbeing, prosperity and solidarity of our citizens. it is very important to communicate, and that cannot be done only by the european institutions or only by member states. we have to do it together in a true spirit of partnership, so a lot of what will happen during these months will be very important for the future of europe, and also for the respect accorded to our institutions all over europe. i would like to use this time to answer some concrete questions put to me, specifically on energy. yes, we need an urgent revision of the gas security directive. the commission said so when it presented its strategic energy review last november, and we are working flat-out to put the legislative proposal before the european parliament and the council as soon as possible. yes, we need more solidarity and more progress in terms of energy security in europe. that was the substance of our strategic energy review presented very recently. i would like to use this time once again to ask all member states and european institutions to work more actively on the mechanisms of european energy solidarity. yes, we also need to accelerate investment in energy infrastructure, interconnectors and energy efficiency. that is why it is so crucial to get the eur 5 billion of the community budget allocated for these purposes. i appeal to you, both parliament and the czech presidency, to translate this into practice as soon as possible. yes, economic recovery needs to be coupled with smart green growth if we want to come out of this crisis stronger. our proposals are on the table to accelerate the use of structural funds for these purposes. funds are needed for energy efficiency and for energy interconnections, and by the coordination of national stimulus programmes we will align national efforts in this direction as well, in line with european council conclusions. let me also say that getting through this economic crisis and putting europe back on the path towards sustainable growth will also require respect for community rules on equal opportunities. europe needs to continue facilitating the participation of both men and women in the economy, as well as helping combine family and professional life. a word also on the lisbon treaty, which many of you mentioned: we need this treaty more than ever, ratified by all member states. yes, we need to respect all national ratification processes, but when a government, on behalf of a state, signs a treaty it takes a solemn commitment to put it into force. (applause)many of you spoke about the czech presidency and how important it is. as some of you said – mr nassauer, mr brok and others – the very fact that we have a czech presidency is in itself an event of great importance. it is the first time a former warsaw pact country has assumed the responsibility of chairing the european council. when you think about it, what a great achievement it is, 20 years after the fall of the iron curtain, to have the czech republic at the head of the european council, and to have prime minister topolánek and vice-prime minister vondra cooperating in this responsibility. i have full confidence in what you are going to do. let me share with you an experience i had last year during the portuguese presidency. prime minister socrates of portugal and i were in zittau, on the border between the czech republic, poland and germany, and i saw the emotion prime minister topolánek was feeling at that moment. that border, which once separated us in europe, between the two sides of europe, is now a free point of circulation among europeans from all those countries. it was a great moment and a great achievement, of which we should be proud. that is why i really believe that it is important to defend our values to make a success of this presidency. some of you said it is important that the czech republic be successful, because it is a small country. i am sorry but the czech republic is not a small country, even in european terms. in fact, it is sometimes a sign of an inferiority complex to consider some of our member states as small countries. let me tell you that – as paul-henri spaak, one of the founders of our european project, said – in the european union there are no longer small and big countries; if you like, none of them is big: the problem is that some of them have not yet noticed it. in fact, when we look at the rest of the world – when we see the dimension of america’s strength in terms of defence and in technology; when we see the huge geographical dimension of russia; when we see the huge demographic dimension of china and india – can we say that there are any big countries in europe? there are no longer big countries in europe. all are great in terms of dignity. for the european commission all the member states have exactly the same dignity, but if we want to be big in the world we need the european union to be strong. we need to act together; we need to use the leverage of all our institutions and, if we act together like that, we will make a difference in the world for good. that is why we need the lisbon treaty. that is why we need a common purpose. that is why we need strong institutions. i wish the czech presidency all the best. (sustained applause)

in writing. the statements by the czech prime minister, the president of the european commission and the debate in the european parliament on the programme of the czech presidency of the european union are a continuation of the anti-people policy of the eu of previous presidencies. they sent the message that the attack of capital against the workers, against the popular strata will continue unabated. the eu's imperialist role will be strengthened, as the czech presidency has also shown from the very beginning of its term of office, by supporting israel's criminal imperialist attack on the gaza strip, by legitimising the barbaric slaughter of the palestinian people, in full agreement with the pa and the pa-nato-eu plan for the 'new middle east'. the programme of the czech presidency marks a continuing intensification of the eu's anti-people offensive, aimed at burdening the consequences of the crisis of the capitalist system on the working class and the poor layers of the population. increasing the eu's aggressiveness against peoples, its imperialist interventions, improving its capacity for military intervention throughout the world. the european union's aggression needs to be met by a counter-attack. resistance, disobedience, a break with the reactionary policy of the eu, with the eu itself, is the way of the people.
in writing. even children should be filed because they are considered potentially dangerous to the eu! this is the proposal of the european commission, the council of the eu and the european parliament. the only difference between the proposal for a directive and the european parliament is the age at which a child is considered dangerous. the commission considers that children become dangerous at the age of six (!) and therefore from that age they must give fingerprints which are to be incorporated into individual passports. the european parliament, on the other hand, in a demonstration of its 'democratic sensitivity', considers that children should be registered from a slightly older age, that is, from 12 years of age! this unacceptable directive, adopted by the supporters of the european road in the european parliament, is the inevitable result of the eu's frantic 'anti-terrorist' policy, which, in order to effectively shield the dominance of capital over the workers' and people's movement, dangerously baptizes even children. it seems that the eu is making good use of the experience of the israeli army defending the 'security' of the israeli state against the palestinian 'terrorists' who are slaughtering countless children in gaza at this very moment. i wonder how old the murdered palestinian girls are. six or 12 years old?
in writing. the pse group in the european parliament voted in favour of the catania report on the situation of fundamental rights in the european union, but hereby emphasises its opposition to the wording of paragraph 49 and the related amendment 35.
in writing. the report attempts to help cover the so-called 'democratic and social deficit' of the eu, which is deeply reactionary in nature, in order to make it more attractive and to soften the resentment caused by its anti-people policy. it accepts and welcomes all the reactionary principles and institutions adopted in the eu, such as the four freedoms of the maastricht treaty, etc., and seeks to give their implementation a more effective dimension. it is based mainly on the eu charter of fundamental rights accompanying the anti-people lisbon treaty, which is below the guaranteed level of rights of many member states. it is - to a large extent - an exhibition of ideas and an abstract ideology, treats fundamental rights such as those of work, education, health, simply as 'opportunities' which must be provided 'equally' to all, something which is of course unrealistic in practice, and, where it becomes more concrete, proposes to deal only with some extreme phenomena of poverty, discrimination, etc. by measures such as the general introduction of a minimum wage, etc. finally, it is revealing that there is no mention of anti-democratic decisions and persecution of citizens, such as the banning of communist parties and other organisations, and the imprisonment of communists and other activists in eu member countries of central europe and the baltic.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the situation of fundamental rights in europe because i believe that it touches on one of the most fundamental problems of our union's construction by making constructive criticism. discrimination in the enjoyment of fundamental rights is mainly the result of the sex, age, origin or sexual orientation of the subjects, as the report rightly points out. the problem is exacerbated when the victims of these violations are unable to respond, mainly because they are confined in mental asylums, juvenile institutions, etc. in the face of this situation, europe cannot remain indifferent, especially since the full respect of human rights will contribute to the consolidation of the european area of freedom and security. in view of the lack of legal binding force of the charter of fundamental rights and the very limited possibility for individuals to have recourse to the community courts, i can only welcome mr catania's proposals for a general obligation for the community institutions to take human rights into account in the performance of their duties and for the creation of a specialised body to this end.
mr president, it is common ground that the situation in gaza is tragic, bordering on a humanitarian catastrophe and requiring immediate action. i would like to congratulate the european commission on its increased efforts, the presidency on its initiatives and the coordination of national mobility in this field, and egypt on the important and sensitive role it is playing. the european union is also calling for a ceasefire in the gaza strip, and for the palestinians to be able to return to their homes and to return to their homes. the commissioner said that there were positive signs for a ceasefire plan and i hope that it will be accepted and respected in its implementation. but what are our next steps? the commissioner and the president-in-office of the council have already said that we must support our objectives of lasting peace and the creation of a palestinian state living in peace and respect alongside israel. these are not new. we have declared them, we have supported them, with no results. the vicious cycle of violence continues with negative consequences not only for the people of israel and the palestinians, but for all the peoples of the region and for the security of the international community. we must now take stock of our actions, our political choices, our practices and take steps that are bolder and different. it is imperative that we have, at bilateral level with israel, an honest, in-depth, self-critical dialogue within the framework of our partnership and friendship, and identify the mistakes made in promoting mutual trust between the two peoples. we must also strengthen such a dialogue with all palestinians, make them understand the importance of peace, cohesion, human life and unity amongst themselves.
mr president, public opinion throughout europe is asking the union for one thing: to put an end to the slaughter of the palestinian people. we must condemn indiscriminate violence wherever it comes from, but we must also be consistent in recognizing that israel is responding with state terrorism on a massive scale. the disproportionate retaliation, the violation of every concept of international and humanitarian law by israel, cannot be tolerated. it is unacceptable to use white phosphorus bombs and other experimental weapons against civilians and it is inhumane to target innocent women and children. if this were to happen in africa or anywhere else in the world, our response would be immediate and the security council's decision binding. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i believe that we must use all the political instruments, including the association agreement, to persuade israel to stop its senseless violence against the palestinian people and its denial of access to humanitarian aid. we cannot be passive spectators, because that makes us complicit in the slaughter. the only solution is an immediate ceasefire and the opening of humanitarian routes to gaza, but also the opening of a dialogue with all sides.
in writing. the joint resolution deliberately turns a blind eye to the causes of israel's aggressive and barbaric war, considering it a response to hamas rocket attacks. it is well known that the war was planned and that the causes lie in the israeli occupation, in israel's refusal to implement the un resolutions on an independent palestinian state with east jerusalem as its capital. it is the result of israel's aggressive policy, with the support of the usa and the eu, of illegal settlements and its refusal to return to the 1967 borders. the resolution, although it refers to the cessation of the war, is equally distant, does not call for any eu measures, not even a freeze on the new privileged relations, to put pressure on israel. it does not condemn israel's aggressive policy, but instead intervenes in the internal problems of the palestinians. the signatory powers call for and agree to a stronger eu role related to its imperialist ambitions in the region. they support the us-nato plan for the 'broader middle east' which the eu has agreed to and which aims at the subjugation of the entire region by the imperialists. with this in mind, the kke does not vote for the joint resolution of the political groups, calling for the strengthening of the anti-imperialist struggle, pointing out that there are no good and bad imperialists.
in writing. i refused to vote in favour of the european parliament's joint resolution on the situation in the gaza strip because, despite its positive aspects, it does not unequivocally condemn the military overreaction leading to a humanitarian catastrophe. the european parliament's condemnation and regret of the attacks on civilians and the obstruction of international humanitarian aid is not enough. the european union must take responsibility for the situation and demand that the israeli aggression be brought to an end once and for all and that it should lead the efforts towards a lasting and sustainable solution. the european parliament has been unable to reach a consensus on the need to establish a european union with a strong political will.
in writing. the joint resolution of the european parliament on srebrenica is an attempt to distort history and to silence or shift responsibility to the victims for the crimes of the us-european imperialists in the former yugoslavia, the dismemberment of the country and the barbaric war launched against it by nato, with the participation of the eu. at the same time, efforts are being made to elevate the status of the unpopular american-inspired special court in the hague, which the imperialists seek to use to try their victims and which was already used for the physical extermination of yugoslav leader slobodan milosevic. the characterization of the srebrenica events as the greatest post-war crime, and even the proposal to establish a memorial day in eu countries, while there are still serious questions about what really happened there, is a gross falsification of history because in fact the greatest post-war crime in europe to date is the massacre of the people of yugoslavia by the us-european imperialists. the kke, refusing to legitimize the adoption of such unacceptable resolutions, at a time when we are all witnessing the daily slaughter of hundreds of children and civilians in palestine by israel with the support of the same imperialist forces that massacred the people of yugoslavia, did not take part in the vote.
mr president, we have heard, read and seen, in recent times, civilians being bombed, children being killed and recently we saw a turkish gentleman who publicly confessed that during the turkish invasion of cyprus in 1974 he murdered 10 greek cypriot soldiers in iron bars. what we have not seen, mr president, is the willingness of the hague criminal court, a willingness which was so obvious to those involved in the events in yugoslavia; they're still being hunted by the court. i think it is therefore legitimate to ask parliament to tell us whether there is a list of countries which fall within the jurisdiction of the hague court or whether there are countries which are indifferent and disregard the hague. i think it is fair to ask which countries and their citizens are to be brought before the hague court as defendants and which are not.
mr president, the rural demonstrations in greece, among other demonstrations in europe, are sending important messages of change in current perceptions of agriculture and the food question, calling for a review of european and national policies. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done on this subject. it is appropriate for the commission and parliament to enter into a dialogue and to put forward proposals to deal with the problems not only of the survival of small and medium-sized farms but also of their exit from the crisis in the coming years, the activation of the intervention mechanism, the strengthening of the crisis management mechanism to cover even loss of income and national policies that can gain more flexibility without this necessarily implying co-financing of the common agricultural policy.
mr president, it took a long time to rid the balkans of sterile nationalisms which are not in line with european standards of behaviour and values. but it seems that some people need to get this message back. in october 2008, the government of the former yugoslav republic of macedonia submitted an application for community financing for the road axis 10 which runs through its territory. just two months later, in a scandalous decision that was even published in the official newspaper of that country, they decided to name this axis "alexander the great of macedonia", in flagrant violation of the interim agreement with greece, which explicitly provides for the avoidance of government propaganda and the use of symbols that contribute to enmity, hatred and violence. i call on the european commission to link the request for community funding to the withdrawal of this scandalous decision which reminds us that there are still nationalists in the balkans. we have built a europe in which nationalism has no place.
madam president, small and mediumsized farmers have been on the streets for 15 days in a struggle that has shaken the whole country. the common agricultural policy, which is jointly formulated and implemented by the greek governments, and the agreements within the world trade organization have led to a reduction in agricultural production, a drastic reduction in the income of small and medium-sized farmers and an acceleration of the eradication of small farmers. greece's agricultural trade balance went from surplus to deficit, reaching about eur 3 billion in 2008 alone. the new democracy government, instead of satisfying the basic demands of small and medium-sized farmers, is trying to deceive them with empty advertisements and, at the same time, is mobilizing special forces to suppress their struggle. the communist party of greece and the workers support the struggle of the small and medium farmers against the common agricultural policy, the entire anti-people policy of the european union and the bourgeois governments, demanding guaranteed lower prices for agricultural and livestock products that cover the production costs and improve the income of small and medium farmers.
member of the commission. − madam president, we are facing the greatest economic challenge for a generation, and in that challenge both developed and developing countries face difficult and very important decisions. we need a positive effect from globalisation to respond to the negative effects of globalisation. it is my strong belief that, wherever we begin to look at what we need to do, we will end in the conclusion that completing the multilateral trade round – the doha round – is of vital importance to us. i probably do not need to remind honourable members of what history teaches us about protectionism, the importance of keeping our markets open and the possibility for our businesses to be able to trade across the world. i probably do not need to tell all honourable members that, simply within the rules of the world trade organisation, if countries moved from applying their tariffs the way that they do at the present time to applying them in the way they are allowed to do under the rules, then the cost to trade would be something of the order of eur 260 billion. i am sure i do not need to remind honourable members that, as the developing nations consider the future, they are deeply concerned about what will happen to some of the aid that has been available to them so far. so, in terms of where we are: we know the value of a deal that is now 80% complete and that in july 2008 80% of what needed to be done was done. within that agreement, the value is of the following order: the gains in the developing countries would be of the order of eur 12-14 billion annually; there would be new access to emerging markets in emerging countries, such as china; we would have the opportunity in the european union for new exports, diversified in new ways – for example, in chemicals and textiles – and in services there is a potential for eur 14 billion of trade. another fact at the present time is that the non-tariff barriers – the non-tax barriers – in china alone in 2007 cost european union companies eur 20 billion. this is a hugely important round. i have just returned from davos, where discussions between trade ministers reinforced the need to get back to the negotiating table and, of course, the technical discussions are continuing in geneva. we all wait for the new american administration to review its trade policies, as it currently plans to do, and to reach the same conclusion that we have reached. we look forward to the g20 on 2 april 2009 and the opportunity it affords world leaders in terms of looking to resolve the financial and economic crisis and having the opportunity to discuss again the need to complete the round. then there are the indian elections in april or may, which of themselves will be the moment for an existing or a new government to return to this subject. of the outstanding issues that were left on the table, there is the special support mechanism, which, in the end, was the issue that prevented is very obvious and very clear, and i look forward, on your behalf – as well as that of the commission – to continuing my efforts to ensure that we do.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the union is, as is well known, the world's largest exporter and the main source of foreign direct investment. the union is one of the most open markets, while our leading partners maintain high trade barriers. we are seeking a substantial reduction in the duties in force and an end to unjustified non-tariff barriers. in addition to the third advanced industrial countries, there are the emerging economies which must make concessions commensurate with their level of development and sectoral competitiveness. the problem, however, is not simply the removal of barriers: differences in regulatory regimes create additional costs for exports, put european products at a competitive disadvantage compared to those imported from countries with more flexible standards and, in many cases, raise safety and protection issues for european consumers. the prolonged failure to reach agreement is exacerbating the climate of economic uncertainty and is a blow to the very credibility of the multilateral trading system. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. furthermore, the economic crisis may trigger the erection of unilateral, restrictive or distorting barriers to trade. the commission has already taken steps to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full, and that the community's financial resources are used to the full. the same worrying trend is being followed by the clause on the protection of american products adopted by the house of representatives. i would say that a return to unilateral approaches is not the answer. today more than ever we need to manage challenges together with more positive integration. by establishing or strengthening international regulatory regimes with systemic convergence. we need a new international economic architecture. we need a more transparent and balanced global trade governance and on this, commissioner, we are waiting for a comprehensive proposal for 'globalisation with a european face' which will take account of the changes already made but also of the interconnection of the commercial and economic dimension for a europe that is transparent, democratic and effective in the world at this time of crisis.
madam president, the strategic choice of the european union and the bourgeois governments, in the midst of the deep capitalist crisis - a crisis of capital accumulation and overproduction - which is also afflicting the member states of the european union, is to use the world trade organization as an important support with an active role in enforcing the full liberalisation of trade and exchanges, privatisations and acquisitions, the penetration of european monopolies into new markets. the doha round talks are aimed at a coordinated all-out attack by capital so that multinational companies can plunder the raw materials of third countries and increase the exploitation of workers throughout the capitalist world. the anti-people common agricultural policy is the exchange for promoting the european union's objectives of liberalising the markets for non-agricultural products and services in order to secure positions in the imperialist pyramid. it is clear that we are interested in international trade and its development on a mutually beneficial basis. but it is impossible, under imperialist conditions, for world trade to be equitable and based on mutual benefit. this is why it is imperative to escalate the workers' struggle in an anti-imperialist, anti-monopoly direction in order to bring about radical changes both internationally and in each country individually.

preferred us to do so. imports from china have increased overall, but within reasonable limits. significant increases in some categories – such as for example dresses, trousers and pullovers – have dimensions. in addition to structural problems, the sharp divergence between the union's import duties on the one hand and those of our major competitors on the other plays a negative role.
